## Document 007: 10月金融数据点评：社融增速回升

Source PDF: all report pdf/10月金融数据点评：社融增速回升.pdf
Report source: 中证鹏元
Date: 2026-11-24
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

中证鹏元资信评估股份有限公司
研究发展部
赵云蛟
zhaoyj@cspengyuan.com
吴进辉
wujh@cspengyuan.com
主要内容：
11 月 13 日，央行公布 2026 年 10 月金融统计数据。总体来看，10 月社融存量增速
企稳回升，信贷总量持续承压，且单月新增规模转负，广义融资结构重塑趋势延续。
社融增量同比多增，主要依赖政府债券拉动，同时表外及直接融资边际改善，8,000
亿政策性金融工具投放带动相关配套资金加速落地，对委托贷款形成支撑。信贷结
构持续分化，微观主体加杠杆意愿依然偏弱。其中企业中长贷在新型政策性金融工
具集中投放支撑下维持相对韧性，而地产贴息政策效果尚未充分体现，居民端资产
负债表延续收缩。监管强化防范资金空转，大幅压降票据融资。货币与存款方面广
义流动性扩张呈现企稳迹象，前期政策资金陆续拨付至相关实体企业，带动企业活
期现金流改善并推升 M1 增速回升。存款端主要是流动性在季初跨部门转移，同时
政府债集中发行与季节性缴税推动财政存款扩张，对实体流动性产生一定的回笼效

应。
整体而言，10 月金融数据反映出资产负债表的自发修复与收缩进程仍在延续，实
体内生复苏动能尚待巩固，但在政策组合拳显效下，全年 GDP 增长目标预计可顺
利实现。叠加四季度权益市场大幅调整，市场避险情绪被进一步放大，微观主体整
体呈现防御收缩态势。政策层面，预计年内继续出台增量举措概率不高，主要依赖
存量政策效能发挥。后续需重点观察前期落地的地产贴息政策对房地产销售端的实
质效果，持续关注政策性金融工具转化为实物工作量后对企业中长期贷款的带动作
用。货币政策方面，政策利率换锚正式落地相当于释放了隐性降息效果，在配合跨
年流动性平稳过渡或者稳定权益市场等诉求下，仍存在降准的可能性。当前市场博
弈的核心将逐步转向对 2027 年的定调，重点关注 12 月的中央经济工作会议。
社融增速回升——10 月金融数据点评 宏观点评
2026 年 11 月 13 日

表 1 10 月金融数据概览

### Page 3

2026 年 11 月 13 日，央行公布 2026 年 10 月金融统计数据。
一、社融：10 月社融存量增速企稳回升，政府债融资依然是核心支撑
2026 年 10 月新增社融 1.14 万亿元，同比多增 3,251 亿元，社融存量增速较上月回升 0.1 个百分点至
7.2%。本月社融总量整体企稳回升，广义融资结构重塑趋势仍在延续。在信贷总量承压的背景下，社融增量
主要依赖政府债券拉动。直接融资累计占比达 53%，宏观融资大盘正逐步降低对传统表内信贷的单一依赖，
政策驱动下的融资结构转型特征持续显现。
分项数据来看，政府债券当月新增 1.18 万亿元，同比多增 6,983 亿元，政府债发行规模在 9 月冲高后
虽有所回落，但仍然同比多增，在化债与稳增长诉求下构成广义融资的核心增量。人民币贷款减少 5,846 亿
元，同比多减 5,692 亿元，实体信贷需求不足依然是社融的主要拖累项。表外及直接融资方面边际改善，委
托贷款当月新增 1,862 亿元，同比多增 208 亿元，10 月底，8,000 亿政策性金融工具全部投放，带动相关配

套资金加速落地，对委托贷款形成支撑。表外未贴现承兑汇票减少 1,687 亿元，同比少减 1,208 亿元，同时
企业债券新增 3,446 亿元，同比多增 946 亿元，在融资环境整体宽松及利率下行周期中实体企业直接债券融
资意愿整体回升。
图1 广义融资结构重塑延续，人民币信贷是主要拖累，政府债是主要支撑
资料来源：Wind，中证鹏元整理

二、信贷：10 月信贷总量承压，政策发力支撑企业中长端维持相对韧性
10 月新增人民币贷款减少 1,000 亿元，同比多减 3,200 亿元。信贷总量相对承压且各部门结构持续分
化，微观主体加杠杆意愿依然偏弱，资产负债表的自发修复与收缩进程仍在延续。当前信贷扩张的动能主要
依赖宏观政策的托底，实体经济内生性的投资与消费需求尚待进一步激发。
居民端信贷当月减少 4,300 亿元，同比多减 696 亿元。其中居民短贷减少 2,700 亿元，同比少减 166 亿
元，尽管 10 月已进入双十一大促阶段，且各地消费品以旧换新及相关贴息政策正陆续落地发力，但实际消
费信贷的整体回升态势依然偏弱，微观主体在收入预期不稳背景下对日常消费加杠杆的意愿普遍审慎，消
费信用扩张动能受到实质性制约。居民中长贷减少 1,563 亿元，同比多减 863 亿元，主要受制于房地产市场
销售端仍在底部调整区间，新增按揭贷款的派生规模有限，10 月开始实施的地产贴息政策对信贷数据的拉

动尚未充分体现，多重因素共同拖累居民端资产负债表扩张。
企业端贷款减少 1,600 亿元，同比多减 5,100 亿元，内部呈现长强短弱特征。企业中长贷新增 1,600 亿
元，同比多增 1,300 亿元，在信贷整体偏弱背景下表现出相对韧性，主要反映了政策端的支撑效果，新型政
策性金融工具在 9 月-10 月集中投放，为重大项目有效补充了资本金，叠加大规模设备更新等产业政策的结
构性引导，直接撬动了银行长端配套信贷的定向投放。企业短贷新增 100 亿元，同比多增 2,000 亿元，实体
企业常规营运资金需求边际企稳。票据融资大幅减少 3,117 亿元，同比多减 8,123 亿元，主要由于监管持续
强化防范资金空转套利，银行通过票据贴现单纯做大信贷规模的行为受到严格规范。近期市场关于票据剥
离信贷属性的预期有所升温，未来若相关统计口径优化落地，票据定价及业务模式将进一步脱离信贷扰动
并回归真实贸易本源。

三、货币与存款：10 月流动性季节性转移，财政存款扩张，M1 增速边际回升
10 月 M1 同比 4.4%，较 9 月回升 1.2 个百分点，M2 同比 7.1%，较 9 月微降 0.1 个百分点。同时社融
存量同比 7.2%，较上月回升 0.1 个百分点。整体看，10 月广义流动性扩张呈现企稳迹象，资金活化程度边
际改善，而存款端则表现出典型的季初跨部门转移及缴税抽水效应。
从货币供给走势看，M1 回升与 M2 微降带动 M2 与 M1 剪刀差由上月的 4.0%收窄至 2.7%。M2 偏弱主
要由表内信贷收缩导致存款派生机制受阻所致。M1 增速的回升主要来源于前期宏观政策资金逐步向下游传
导，9 月至 10 月集中投放的新型政策性金融工具资金以及前期发行的政府债券资金陆续拨付至相关实体企
业的项目账户，直接带动了企业活期现金流的边际改善。社融增速回升且与 M2 剪刀差间隔 10 个月转正至

0.1%，进一步确认广义流动性扩张的企稳态势。

### Page 8

存款方面，10 月新增人民币存款 1.21 万亿元，同比多增 6,000 亿元，主要是季初流动性跨部门转移。
居民存款减少 9,200 亿元，同比少减 4,200 亿元，企业存款减少 1.25 万亿元，同比多减 1,647 亿元。与实体
部门存款减少相对应，非银机构存款增加 1.58 万亿元，同比少增 2,700 亿元，非银资金规模的扩张主要受
季初理财资金回流非银体系拉动。此外 10 月财政存款增加 1.4 万亿元，同比多增 6,800 亿元，缴税大月，
叠加政府债集中发行导致财政存款账面大幅扩张，财政资金阶段性沉淀对实体流动性形成一定的回笼效应，
后续财政资金能否加速下拨将关系到 M1 增速回升的持续性。
图11 季初流动性跨部门转移，财政存款大幅扩张

总体来看，10 月社融存量增速企稳回升，信贷总量持续承压，且单月新增规模转负，广义融资结构重
塑趋势延续。社融增量同比多增，主要依赖政府债券拉动，同时表外及直接融资边际改善，8,000 亿政策性
金融工具投放带动相关配套资金加速落地，对委托贷款形成支撑。信贷结构持续分化，微观主体加杠杆意愿
依然偏弱。其中企业中长贷在新型政策性金融工具集中投放支撑下维持相对韧性，而地产贴息政策效果尚
未充分体现，居民端资产负债表延续收缩。监管强化防范资金空转，大幅压降票据融资。货币与存款方面广
义流动性扩张呈现企稳迹象，前期政策资金陆续拨付至相关实体企业，带动企业活期现金流改善并推升 M1
增速回升。存款端主要是流动性在季初跨部门转移，同时政府债集中发行与季节性缴税推动财政存款扩张，
对实体流动性产生一定的回笼效应。
整体而言，10 月金融数据反映出资产负债表的自发修复与收缩进程仍在延续，实体内生复苏动能尚待
巩固，但在政策组合拳显效下，全年 GDP 增长目标预计可顺利实现。叠加四季度权益市场大幅调整，市场

避险情绪被进一步放大，微观主体整体呈现防御收缩态势。政策层面，预计年内继续出台增量举措概率不
高，主要依赖存量政策效能发挥。后续需重点观察前期落地的地产贴息政策对房地产销售端的实质效果，持
续关注政策性金融工具转化为实物工作量后对企业中长期贷款的带动作用。货币政策方面，政策利率换锚
正式落地相当于释放了隐性降息效果，在配合跨年流动性平稳过渡或者稳定权益市场等诉求下，仍存在降
准的可能性。当前市场博弈的核心将逐步转向对 2027 年的定调，重点关注 12 月的中央经济工作会议。

2026 年 11 月 9 日

レポート

2026 年冬のボーナス見通し

～企業の好業績と人手不足を背景に堅調な増加が続く～

調査部 主席研究員 小林 真一郎 
研究員 小林 啓介

○ 2026 年冬の民間企業（調査産業計・事業所規模 5 人以上）のボーナスは、前年比＋5.0%と 6 年連続で増
加が見込まれる。好調な企業業績と堅調な雇用情勢が追い風となり、大幅な伸びになるだろう。 
○ 支給労働者割合は 87.3%（前年差＋0.8%ポイント）と 2 年ぶりに上昇するだろう。同割合はコロナ前の水準
を超えて回復しており、雇用者数の増加が続く中、ボーナスが支給される事業所で働く労働者の数は 4,577
万人（前年比＋1.9%）と、4 年連続で過去最多を更新する見込みである。 
○ 一人当たり支給額と支給労働者数の増加を受け、ボーナスの支給総額は 20.4 兆円（前年比＋7.0%）と 6 年
連続で増加しよう。ボーナスの支給総額の伸びは物価上昇率を大きく上回っており、個人消費の回復を下
支えすると期待される。 
○ 2026 年冬の国家公務員（管理職および非常勤を除く一般行政職）のボーナス（期末・勤勉手当）の平均支
給額は 77 万 9,100 円（前年比＋4.4%）と前年冬に続き、大きめの増加が見込まれる。景気回復に伴う民間
給与の増加が公務員にも反映される状況が続き、基本給、ボーナス支給月数とも引き上げられるとみられる。 
2026 年冬のボーナス見通し

(注 1)民間企業（調査産業計・事業所規模 5 人以上）は、賞与を支給する事業所で働く全常用労働者（当該事業所で賞与
の支給を受けていない労働者も含む）の平均 
(注 2)国家公務員は、管理職および非常勤を除く一般行政職の平均 
(注 3)支給労働者数は、賞与を支給する事業所で働く全常用労働者（当該事業所で賞与の支給を受けていない労働者も
含む）の数 
(注 4)支給総額は一人当たり支給額に支給労働者数を掛け合わせた値 
(出所)厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計」、内閣人事局資料などを基に当社予測

1. 2026 年夏のボーナス ～企業の好業績を背景に 5 年連続の増加

厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計調査」によると、民間企業（調査産業計・事業所規模 5 人以上）における 2026
年夏のボーナスの一人当たり支給額は、前年比＋5.3%と 5 年連続で増加した（図表 1）。前年（2025 年）の
伸びである同＋2.9%を大きく上回っており、2026 年は企業の好業績を背景にボーナスは堅調に増加した。 
業種別では、製造業（前年比＋5.8%）、非製造業（同＋5.3%）ともに増加した。内訳をみると、生活関連サ
ービス等（前年比＋25.7%）、建設業（同＋14.4%）、学術研究等（同＋12.9%）などが大幅に増加し、全体
の数字を押し上げた。一方で、複合サービス事業（同－2.1%）は減少した。 
ボーナス支給月数（所定内給与に対する比率）は 1.10 カ月分と前年から 0.02 カ月増加した。また、ボー
ナスが支給された労働者の割合 1は 85.1%（前年差＋0.5%ポイント）と小幅に上昇した。雇用者数が順調に
増加して過去最高水準を更新しており、ボーナスが支給された労働者数は前年比＋1.4%と増加した。夏の
ボーナスの支給総額（平均給与額×支給労働者数）は 19.9 兆円（前年比＋6.8%）と過去最高を記録した。

める割合。

2. 2026 年冬のボーナス見通し

(1) 民間企業 ～ 企業の好業績と労働需給の逼迫を追い風に、6 年連続で増加する見込み

厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計調査」ベースで見た民間企業（調査産業計・事業所規模 5 人以上）の 2026 年冬
のボーナスの一人当たり平均支給額は 44 万 6,252 円（前年比＋5.0%）と 6 年連続での増加を予想する（図表
2）。企業規模を問わず、製造業、非製造業とも一人当たり平均支給額は増加すると予測する。 
今年の冬のボーナスが順調な増加を続けるとみられる背景に、企業業績の好調さと雇用情勢の堅調さがある。
企業の経常利益（全規模、金融保険業を除く全産業、季節調整値）は、2026 年 4～6 月期に過去最高を更新す
るなど好調に推移している。今後イラン情勢の展開次第では業績に下押し圧力がかかるリスクはあるが、冬のボ
ーナスへの影響は軽微だろう。 
また、人手不足は深刻化し、労働需給は逼迫している。日銀短観における雇用人員判断 DI は対面型サービ
ス業を中心に大幅な「不足」超が続き、企業の人手不足感が強まる中、完全失業率は 2％台半ばと低水準での推
移が続いている。

（注 1）調査産業計、事業所規模 5 人以上  
（注 2）支給月数は所定内給与に対する支給割合 
（出所）厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計」

(2) 公務員 ～ 基本給、支給月数ともプラスに寄与し、ボーナスは 5 年連続で増加

内閣人事局の発表によると、国家公務員（管理職および非常勤を除く一般行政職）の 2026 年夏のボーナス
（期末・勤勉手当）は 73 万 8,500 円（前年比＋4.5%）と 4 年連続で増加した。昨年の人事院勧告に基づく給与
法改正でボーナスの基準となる基本給が前年比＋3.3%と増加したほか、ボーナス支給月数が昨夏から 0.025 カ
月引き上げられた。 
2026 年冬のボーナスの平均支給額は 77 万 9,100 円（前年比＋4.4%）と、5 年連続で増加すると予測する（図
表 8）。今年の人事院勧告に沿った給与法改正が行われることを前提に、平均給与額が増加（＋3.3%）するととも
に、ボーナス支給月数が昨冬から 0.025 カ月分（勤勉手当、期末手当がそれぞれ 0.0125 カ月分）引き上げられ
ると想定する。支給額は夏冬通じて過去最高を更新し、景気回復に伴う民間給与の増加が公務員にも反映され
る状況が続くとみられる。今後も、民間の賃金上昇傾向が続く中、公務員のボーナスも高めの伸びが続く見込み
である。 
図表 8 冬のボーナス予測（国家公務員）

回归均衡，谋定而后动 
报告导读： 
我们的观点：2027 年美股或延续内部结构再平衡，非AI 资产广度扩散有望支撑指数韧性，结构性波动仍大于指数整体波动。
AI 和通胀构成两大核心矛盾：AI 产业景气度定全年增长斜率，通胀&利率定全年交易节奏

我们的逻辑：增长斜率和流动性的时间之争：（1）分子端-2027 年美股盈利增长动能或边际放缓。结构层面：2027 年美国
AI 与非 AI 板块间的 K 型分化有望收敛，周期和科技对于美股盈利增长的贡献趋于降速，盈利动能贡献有望更均衡。（2）
分母端：宏观流动性先紧后松，AI 信用局部风险暴露。

（正文） 
1.  2026 年海外权益行情回顾：市场从极致 K 型分化走向结构性再平衡 
1.1   “AI 抱团-瓦解-再平衡”行情极致演绎：26 年美股更多为结构性波动，指数总体具备韧性 
回顾2026 年，美股经历了“AI 抱团-瓦解-再平衡”行情的极致演绎，概括而言，美股指数韧性强于个股、
结构波动大于总量波动。上半年，地缘冲突压制宏观风险偏好，AI 产业景气构成基本面狭窄的繁荣，市场缩圈
至业绩确定性最强的AI 硬件资产。下半年，AI 下游商业化增长动能边际放缓叠加市场对CSP 资本开支可持续
性及投资回报的质疑，前期极致抱团的AI 行情逐步瓦解，拥挤的筹码结构放大了波动。与此同时，若排除地
缘扰动的外生影响，非AI 资产亦能展现较强的韧性，实现更广泛的行业扩散，一定程度对冲了AI 板块调整的
负面影响，使得指数整体波动相对收敛。 
图 1：26 年美股更多为结构性波动，指数总体具备韧性  图 2：上半年 AI 资产贡献标普 500 上涨主要驱动，下
半年非 AI 资产再平衡对冲科技下跌对于指数的拖累

资料来源：彭博，国泰君安期货研究  资料来源：彭博，国泰君安期货研究

图 3：若排除地缘扰动的外生影响，非 AI 资产亦能展
现较强的承接韧性，实现更广泛的行业扩散 
 图 4：若排除地缘扰动的外生影响，非 AI 资产亦能展
现较强的承接韧性，实现更广泛的行业扩散

资料来源：彭博，国泰君安期货研究  资料来源：彭博，国泰君安期货研究

图 5：上半年极致的动量效应（AI 硬件主要贡献指数上涨驱动），下半年极致的动量反转（AI 硬件主要拖累指
数上涨驱动）

资料来源：彭博，国泰君安期货研究

1.2  行情核心驱动：AI 产业景气趋势主导方向，抱团交易瓦解放大波动 
2026 上半年：AI 产业景气突破成为核心驱动，盈利增长消化估值压力。上半年，尽管地缘冲突扰动持续
压制宏观风险偏好，但AI Agent（Anthropic）应用场景取得突破性进展进一步强化AI 产业稀缺的景气度，
较强的盈利增长对前期估值形成消化和支撑，驱动AI 板块上涨。

2026 下半年：AI 产业潜在风险逐步显现，市场对于AI 产业叙事的预期开始松动，杀估值构成行情调整
的核心驱动，此外，资金面微观结构恶化放大波动。2026Q3 以来，市场基本完成了对于“AI 二阶导（增速）”
反转的确认，而对于“AI 一阶导（增长）”反转更多停留在预期层面质疑的加深而非现实端的确认。因此，从
DDM 拆解来看，下半年全球科技股调整更多表现为杀估值而非杀业绩。

2.  分子端：增长由高波动转向温和收敛，盈利增长降速，K 型分化收敛

总量层面：2027 年美股盈利增长动能或边际放缓。结构层面：2027 年美国AI 与非AI 板块间的 K 型分化
有望收敛，周期和科技对于美股盈利增长的贡献趋于降速，盈利动能贡献有望更均衡。具体而言：（1）科技板
块：高基数效应下，若下游商业化兑现不及预期或进展偏慢，AI 算力链周期属性强化，2027 年美股科技板块
盈利增长降速是中性假设；（ 2）能源板块：地缘和油价是核心变量，高基数效应下2027 年盈利增长趋于降
速；（3）传统板块：财政脉冲由支撑转为温和拖累，退税规模较2026 年的高位回落，房地产、传统资本开支
与低收入消费修复节奏更多取决于通胀和宏观流动性。

2.1  宏观：增长由高波动转向温和收敛 
增长由高波动转向温和收敛，通胀有望趋势回落，K 型分化收敛。2027 年美国实际GDP 季环比折年率四个
季度分别预计为2.0%、2.1%、2.1%和2.2%，全年均值约2.1%，较2026 年的2.5%回落0.4 个百分点；但季
度间振幅由2026 年的3.2 个百分点降至0.2 个百分点。2026 年的高波动源于政府停摆后的基数扰动、关税与
能源价格冲击以及AI 投资的一次性脉冲，这些因素在2027 年基本淡出，增长回到2%上下的潜在增速附近。
以四季度同比衡量，2027 年预期为2.1%，较2026 年同期的2.3%小幅回落0.2 个百分点，全年同比在2.0%—
2.3%之间，呈"中枢微降、路径平坦"的形态。驱动来看，财政脉冲由支撑转为温和拖累，退税规模较2026 年
的高位回落；房地产、传统资本开支与低收入消费受高利率压制更为明显；AI 相关投资仍在贡献增量，但边际
增速回落。 
图 13：美国主要宏观经济指标展望及美联储 9 月 SEP 展望（26Q4 后为预期值）

资料来源：国泰君安期货研究

2.1.1  中期选举后政经背景变化及对市场的影响 
展望前方，经济议题将是2027 年乃至2028 年大选年中最关键的政治博弈战场，“降低通胀”和“弥合经
济K 分化”将是持续性的政治诉求——分别对应着降低油价的天然诉求和对AI 板块的监管、再分配诉求。 
本次中期选举点出美国政经气候变化最大的特征，是选民焦点已经从意识形态的“左右”之争转为经济阶
层的“上下”之争、从文化之争转为经济之争，“K 型分化”最终清晰的显示在投票版图中，成为民主党的
Tailwind。在“由红转蓝”的部分关键摇摆州，民主党候选人以一种对“意识形态”分歧较为克制和中立的态
度，但火力全开直指降低物价等经济诉求。在过去数十年的选举周期中，“左右”之争中如移民问题、少数群
体、宗教、“美国至上”（MAGA）、去精英化等议题，在当前高物价、K 型分化、贫富分化等经济失衡加剧的背景
下再度煽动意识形态武器已然不合时宜，而共和党候选人在应对经济议题时显然无法与特朗普政府进行切割。
事实上，民主党正在获得部分MAGA 选票，2024 共和党的MAGA 群体与2026 民主党的劳动人民群体在经济逻辑
上并没有那么大差异。蓝色狂潮必然会阻碍科技和AI 进展？我们不这么认为，基本面逻辑而非政经变化仍旧
是我们前期相对看空科技板块的主因，选举只是阶段性强化这一方向。但是必须要看到“Technocracy”或者
“科技寡头-政治”的联盟已经成为当前经济背景下的对立面。

### Page 8

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分 
 8 
图 14：2026 年美国中期选举选民议题关注度排序

资料来源：国泰君安期货研究

落到对金融市场影响看，美国政经变化如何影响中短期市场，哪些领域政策变化概率较大、哪些领域实质
性影响不大？——我们从三类路径看可为和不可为。一是不依赖新立法、总统可自行决定：如232、301 关税
已在实际执行，对华301 条款10 月7 日确认继续有效；先进芯片出口许可与对外投资审查已有法律基础（《综
合对外投资国家安全法》）、军事行动属总统权限——无太大变化，国会能做的只有拨款附加条件、听证和诉
讼，属于降低行政执行效率的施压手段。二是需要新立法+参议院60 票+总统签署：如企业税率从21%上调至
25%—28%、恢复ACA 增强补贴与联邦医保补贴、恢复被削减的IRA 清洁能源税惠、收回总统关税授权等——民
主党推行自己的议程并非易事，对当前市场主线影响不大。普通法案在参议院需60 票才能结束辩论，推翻否
决需两院各三分之二多数。三是必须通过的法案与参议院专属权力：如12 月11 日到期的临时拨款（覆盖全部
12 项年度拨款）、已在众议院以216:212 通过的2027 财年国防授权法、延至12 月11 日的《国防生产法》授
权、2027 年债务上限；以及参议院人事确认权（美联储理事、联邦法官、内阁）与众议院传票、监督权等。—
—重要财政变化和显著债务扩张难度仍大，但我们会更关注年底债务上限博弈和政府停摆风险。

图 15：参议院多数立法需 60 票（预算协调除外），且总统可否决，因此民主党掌控一院或两院，主要作用是阻
止共和党议程，而非推行自己的议程

2.1.2  地缘-油价-通胀情景如何演变？通胀依赖同比基数效应

我们认为2027 年美国通胀趋势有所回落、中枢下降。但通胀趋势依赖同比基数，且近端2026Q4-2027Q1
通胀读数仍旧较高，2027Q2 同比基数效应显现。 
当前中东地缘形式总体看既保留谈判通道但局势也未实质性降温。2026 年2 月底美伊冲突爆发以来，霍
尔木兹海峡通行受伊朗方面制约，成为双方博弈的核心筹码。美国中期选举和以色列大选关键转折，美国中期
选举中民主党夺回参众两院，以色列方面内塔尼亚胡阵营失去议会多数，两者强化了冲突降温预期。外交层
面，卡塔尔、巴基斯坦持续斡旋，伊朗提出以美方解除制裁、解冻约80 亿美元资产为条件、两天内重开海峡
的“两天计划”，伊朗扬称最高领袖已下令结束“非战非和”状态、愿以谈判破局。但实质进展尚未落地，伊朗
同时表示并未与美国直接谈判，11 月以来美军对伊朗仍有“小型打击”；伊朗革命卫队解除导弹射程限制、美
军向阿曼湾货船开火；海峡接连发生油轮触雷起火与爆炸事件，沙特与胡塞武装的互袭亦未停止。 
对于原油市场而言，我们的观点是“去繁存简”，中长期仍旧视作宏观风险，一方面，主要因地缘核心矛盾
未解决，使得供给持续回归具有脆弱性（伊朗仍有袭击海峡油轮的能力、胡塞武装仍有干扰沙特原油供应能
力），原油价格持续蕴含地缘溢价，低库存水平亦支持原油估值。但另一方面，中东供给流量逐步向战前水平
恢复、运费价格有所回落、下游需求对高油价负反馈、高油价高通胀下的政治压力等也真实抑制油价上行空
间。我们按照原油75-110 美元/桶宽幅区间震荡，来对2027 年通胀水平和趋势做测算。 
我们大致测算，油价每变动10 美元/桶，2027 年CPI 同比约变动0.35 个百分点。基准情形下：75 美元
对应约1.97%，85 美元对应约2.32%，95 美元对应约2.68%，100 美元对应约2.85%，110 美元对应约
3.21%。不过，此模型仅仅考虑“油价-汽油-通胀权重-通胀”的传导路径，未能考虑1）战略储备释放、燃油
税调整等政策对冲；2）成品油裂解价差大幅走阔风险带来更强的通胀传导；3）工资—价格螺旋与通胀预期；
4）下游需求负反馈程度。 
另外，归总市场一致预期、彭博经济学家预测、市场定价和BE Model 四方预测美国CPI 同比在2026Q4-
2027Q1 维持在3.0-3.8%区间，在2027Q2 回落至2.1-2.9%区间，在2027Q3-2027Q4 回落至1.9-2.6%区

2.2  科技：年内估值下杀已基本消化 AI 产业降速的预期，2027 年更多面临现实端盈利的考验

自2025Q4 开始，“AI 交易”的风险点逐步浮出，我们大致构建了对AI 产业风险定位的框架：①一阶段
（杀估值）是在AI 下游商业化“增速”见顶放缓后对“AI 二阶导”反转的确认（对应6-8 月全球科技板块调整
的底层逻辑）；②二阶段是在中游CSP 投资回报率回落&自由现金流转负&债务融资信用风险攀升背景下对于远
期Capex 持续性预期层面质疑的加深（对应9 月以来海外股债背离加深的底层逻辑）； ③三阶段（杀业绩）是
在上游硬件价格松动、上游营收和盈利“增长”见顶回落后对于“AI 一阶导”反转的确认（尚未出现，2027
年可能逐步确认）。

展望2027 年，AI 交易的风险点随时间累积：两种情景—AI 下游商业化场景进一步兑现 OR 海外股债非对
称定价逐渐收敛。2026Q3 以来，市场基本完成了对于“AI 二阶导（增速）”反转的确认，而对于“AI 一阶导
（增长）”反转更多停留在预期层面质疑的加深而非现实端的确认。因此，从DDM 拆解来看，下半年全球科技
股调整更多表现为杀估值而非杀业绩。展望2027 年，两种情景—AI 下游商业化场景进一步兑现 OR 股债非对
称定价逐渐收敛：（ 1）乐观情景下，AI 下游实现更广义的商业化兑现（AI Agent&物理AI）—>AI 投资链实现
闭环（估值修复，业绩增长）；（2）悲观情景下，逐渐确认“AI 一阶导”反转（Capex 下修/上游硬件价格崩塌）
——>市场担忧从AI 产业增速见顶（杀估值）—>AI 产业增长见顶（杀业绩）。 
图 18：股债定价背离定位 AI 投资周期进入苛刻的下半场：25Q4 以来海外信用市场率先定价 Capex 融资持
续性与回报约束的风险，而海外权益市场定价明显隐含非对称风险

资料来源：彭博，国泰君安期货研究

### Page 12

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分 
 12 
图 19：2026Q3 以来：市场基本完成了对于“AI 二阶导”反转的确认，而对于“AI 一阶导”反转更多停留
在预期层面质疑的加深而非现实端的确认

资料来源：彭博，国泰君安期货研究

2.2.1  CSP“ROIC-WACC”临界点渐近？AI 下游兑现与上游投资之间的“时差”是当前产业核心瑕疵 
北美云计算厂商ROIC-WACC 的临界点持续逼近：过去一年，北美 CSP 的ROIC-WACC 持续收敛，
Neocloud 的ROIC-WACC 几乎已全线倒挂。 
AI 上游 Capex 维持高强度&下游兑现放缓（ROIC↓）： 核心矛盾在于AI 下游需求兑现速度尚未充分匹配上
游基础设施投资扩张速度（26Q3 Anthropic ARR 放缓进一步激化矛盾）；另一方面，AI 投资下半场，前期巨
额Capex 投入对于利润的侵蚀正在逐步显性化，资产负债表对于下游需求兑现的容错率降低。 
AI 产业信用溢价走廓&宏观利率高企（WACC↑）： 一方面，信用利差走廓反映信用市场对于AI 投资风险的
重新定价：Capex 持续性与回报约束的焦虑加深&供给端恶化（科技企业大规模发债）；另一方面，地缘反复抬
高油价&通胀&海外宏观利率中枢亦间接抬升科技企业融资成本中枢。

2.2.2  Capex 持续性的质疑是否会从预期走向现实？CSP 现金流的约束在显性化，对下游兑现容错率降低 
去年Q4 以来海外股债定价开始背离，今年9 月以来海外股债定价背离加深的底层逻辑：市场对AI 资本开
支可持续性的担忧，正在从对未来盈利与投资回报的预期博弈逐步转向对现实融资约束和资产负债表承载能力
的审视。截至2026Q3，北美 CSP 延续高强度 AI 资本开支，除微软外自由现金流均转负（需依靠外部融资维
持Capex），CSP 现金流约束正在逐步显性化，意味着后续对下游兑现容错率进一步降低。 
图 29：北美 CSP 延续高强度 AI 资本开支，除微软外自由现金流均转负（需依靠外部融资维持 Capex）

2.2.3  Cycle 还是Super Cycle？若下游需求放缓难以证伪，AI 基建定价体系回归周期属性 
若AI 下游商业化放缓、AI 上游投资回报承压等风险持续累积且难以证伪，市场对AI 基础设施的定价逻
辑或将从超级周期（Super Cycle）逐步回归传统资本开支周期（Cycle）：2027 年AI 基建增长降速是中性假
设。历史经验，北美CSP 及费城半导体估值下修往往领先EPS 增速下修1-3 季度。 
自2025Q4 开始，“AI 交易”的风险点逐步浮出，我们大致构建了对AI 产业风险定位的框架：①一阶段
（杀估值）是在AI 下游商业化“增速”见顶放缓后对“AI 二阶导”反转的确认（对应6-8 月全球科技板块调整
的底层逻辑）；②二阶段是在中游CSP 投资回报率回落&自由现金流转负&债务融资信用风险攀升背景下对于远
期Capex 持续性预期层面质疑的加深（对应9 月以来海外股债背离加深的底层逻辑）； ③三阶段（杀业绩）是
在上游硬件价格松动、上游营收和盈利“增长”见顶回落后对于“AI 一阶导”反转的确认（尚未出现）。关
注：（1）GPU 租赁价格& HBM/DRAM 合约及现货价格；（ 2）头部LLM ARR 环比增速；（3）GPU 及服务器订单砍
单/延迟提货风险；（4）AI 融资链金融违约风险（主体评级、CDS 利差、IPO 认购倍数）；（5）Capex 预期是否
下修等维度。

2.2.4  蓝色浪潮对于 AI 产业实质性影响几何？供给端因素更多影响节奏，需求端兑现决定产业方向

蓝色浪潮一定程度强化了地方层面对AI 基础设施投资建设的政策约束。随着民主党在地方选举中取得进
展，部分州及地方政府可能加强对数据中心建设的监管，重点涉及电力消耗、环保标准、土地使用、税收优惠
及公用事业成本等方面，因此可能推高数据中心建设及运营成本，延长项目审批周期，并对新增产能的落地节
奏形成约束。 
整体而言，政策约束更多作用于AI 基础设施的供给侧，可能影响资本开支的落地节奏、建设成本及区域
布局，而AI 下游需求端兑现最终决定产业方向。一方面，相关限制措施因州而异，且多数重大政策调整仍需
经过立法、监管及行政审批程序，实际落地存在一定时滞；另一方面，已经开工或完成前期审批的项目短期受
到的影响相对有限，CSP 也可以通过调整选址、迁移项目及增加能源配套投资等方式应对。

3.  分母端：宏观流动性先紧后松，AI 信用局部风险暴露

3.1  宏观流动性：我们总体判断起点偏紧，全年先紧后松 
2027 年宏观流动性对整体宏观情绪定调有重要影响，我们总体判断起点偏紧，全年先紧后松。当前美国
金融条件已处于边际收紧态势，主要来自利率上行、美元指数走强、信用利差走阔和美股下行多种原因共振。 
美联储预计在12 月和明年3 月各加息25bp 至4.25%-4.50%，此后按兵不动。也有观点认为降息最早在
2027 年11 月启动。因此，全年大致是上半年维持偏紧、下半年视利率回落程度、股票市场和信用市场以及美
元指数从“危机模式”中走出，带动整体金融条件指数逐步转松。 
分项看2027 年宏观流动性： 
一方面看货币政策，上半年仍旧处在升息曲线中，对宏观流动性有所限制，三季度触峰回落对宏观流动性
边际贡献宽松，对加息结束的定价或在通胀显著回落或油价明显下行的可能性下重新开启。当前市场预计
2026 年12 月会议加息约1 次，此后加息预期继续累积，2027 年1 月底加息一次，隐含利率约4.27%；至3
月再度加息，隐含利率升至4.4%以上。加息定价在二季度明显放缓，2027 年7 月至9 月前后见顶，峰值隐含
利率约4.55%，相当于较当前再上行约65bp 左右。2027 三季度后，曲线小幅回落，至2027 年11 月至12 月
隐含利率回落至4.45%左右。这说明市场预期本轮加息在2027 年年中结束，下半年开始计入小幅降息，但全
年政策利率仍明显高于当前水平。 
另一方面看金融条件如股票市场、信用市场和美元指数，整体上看，当前我们认为股票市场调整在中期尚
未结束，而信用市场尤其是科技板块自由现金流恶化预计延续至2027 年中，信用利差或对金融条件指数构成
拖累，美元指数跟随利差逻辑和市场风偏较难判断，但预计美元指数在2027Q1 维持相对强势，对金融条件构
成抑制，而后回落对边际改善金融条件。

3.2  信用风险：AI 产业融资循环链条局部信用风险值得动态关注 
3.2.1  AI 基建投资正在明显增加产业链相关公司对债务融资的依赖 
据IMF 数据：北美云服务商在全球投资级债券发行中的份额从2024 年的不足1%升至2026 年的约10%。
虽然其核心业务盈利强劲，但持续高额的Capex 投资使得FCF 快速下降（除微软外均已转负），AI 基建投资正
在明显增加对债务融资的依赖。而Neocloud 等企业通常负债更高、盈利能力更弱，可能成为AI 生态体系中的
薄弱环节。

 22

3.2.2  AI 循环融资系统（Circular Financing）加大了债务、股权和未来收入之间的相关性

据《Urbanomics ：Update on the AI spending boom》分析：AI 融资体系可能存在三类相互重叠的
风险——循环融资（Circular Financing）、供应商融资（Vendor Financing）和表外杠杆（Off-
balance-sheet Leverage）。它们并非相互独立的融资交易，而是横跨七类产业参与者、相互关联的信用风
险结构。 
从融资机制看，AI 产业正在形成资金循环，使得原本相互独立的双边交易，演变为具有自我强化机制的融
资体系。芯片厂商及Hyperscalers 向AI Lab 提供股权投资，AI Lab 随后将获得的资金转化为对芯片厂商和
云服务商的采购承诺，这些采购承诺进一步形成供应商的在手订单（Backlog）并支撑其债券融资；与此同
时，金融机构通过SPV 和私人信贷为整个AI 基础设施建设提供资金。 
AI 融资体系可能存在三类相互重叠的风险：（1）AI 循环融资可能放大表观需求。（2）关键主体的风险可
能沿AI 产业链同步传导，原本分散在不同环节的风险实际上依赖于同一底层需求假设。如果OpenAI 无法履行
相关承诺，Oracle/CoreWeave 的在手订单、NVIDIA 与AMD 的销售预期，以及SPV 贷款人的抵押品价值，都
可能同时受到重新定价。（3）风险可能向透明度较低的融资渠道转移（私人信贷和SPV 等结构），近两年私人
信贷市场的杠杆与流动性风险持续上升。 
总体而言，只有当AI 需求不及预期时，相关风险才可能集中显现，但由于七类参与者实际上共同承担对
AI 需求增长的押注，一旦需求明显放缓，相关风险可能在多个环节同步暴露，潜在冲击可能通过企业、金融机
构及居民资产负债表向金融市场和实体经济扩散。 
图 49：AI 循环融资（Circular Financing）的七类交易主体

资料来源：Urbanomics，国泰君安期货研究

展望2027 年：美国尚未出现全面信用收缩的典型特征，但AI 链条局部信用及流动性脆弱性有所累积。当
前，美国银行体系资本充足率仍接近历史较高水平，企业与居民债务占GDP 比重持续回落，整体债务脆弱性及
信用风险相对可控，尚未出现全面信用收缩的典型特征，但AI 产业链条循环融资体系内结构性风险值得关

4.  美股结论与投资展望：增配价值，回归均衡，谋定而后动

市场判断：2027 年美股或延续内部结构再平衡，非AI 资产广度扩散有望支撑指数韧性，结构性波动仍大
于指数整体波动，AI 产业景气度定全年增长弹性，通胀&利率定全年交易节奏。 
战略层面：增配价值与防御，回归均衡配置。我们去年以来持续提示的AI 产业四阶段框架，2027 年大致
处于第二阶段向第三阶段逐步过渡的窗口：建议降低AI 硬件，增配必选消费、医疗保健、公用事业等低AI 相
关性资产，回归盈利质量与现金流稳定性增强组合韧性。更长期的视角（第四阶段）， AI 下游（CSP/应用/软
件/物理AI 等）将成为主线资产（目前仍然处于左侧观察区间）。 
战术层面：顺势博弈通胀缓和与利率回落驱动的修复行情。若通胀改善、利率中枢下行且经济保持韧性，
金融条件改善有望催化房地产、可选消费等传统型利率敏感资产+医疗保健代表的成长型利率敏感资产的阶段性
反弹。

### Page 3

WinWay Technology (6515.TW)
26 November 2026 Citi Research
3
WinWay Technology
Valuation
We value WinWay using a PE-based approach that factors in earnings momentum and likely expansion of its addressable
market. Our target price of NT$6,500 is derived from applying c.30x to 2H27E-1H28E EPS, in line with its historical average PE
since 2023, which we believe is justified given the company's entrenched position in the high-end AI GPU testing supply chain,

rising contribution from coaxial socket and SLT solutions, and the increasing value of test content per module. With these
structural drivers in place, and as we believe the market is starting to value it as an AI/HPC play (rather than a consumer
electronics stock), we expect the multiple to hold at a premium relative to its historical averages.
Risks
Our quant rating system rates the WinWay stock High Risk based on its historical volatility.
Key downside risks that could impede the stock from reaching our target price and our earnings forecasts include: 1) client and

sector concentration – specifically any delays in US AI GPU product roadmap could impact the business outlook for WinWay; 2)
intensifying competition in coaxial and hypersockets from global peers; 3) faster-than-expected testing algorithm
optimization, which could shorten testing durations and reduce the anticipated hardware demand multiplier; and 4) delay of
MEMS Phase 2 mass production.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds

2026.11.02   
기아 (000270)

26.3Q Review: 수익성에 대한 입증이 필요  Company Brief

26.3Q Review: 매출액 31.6조원, 영업이익 1.8조원  
기아는 26.3Q 매출액 31.6조원 (+10.1% YoY, -4.4% QoQ), 영업이익 1.8조원 
(+24.3% YoY, -30.8% QoQ, +5.8% OPM) 등의 실적을 기록했다. 26.3Q 영업이익은 
시장 컨센서스 (2.36조원)를 22.9% 하회했다 [iM증권 추정치: 매출액 31.5조원, 
영업이익 2.3조원]. 주요 구성 요소별 내용은 아래와 같다. 
① P: 26.3Q 26.3Q 전동화 차량 판매는 30.0만대 (+47% YoY, 판매 비중 
36.2%)를 기록했는데, 이 중 HEV 17.2만대 (+46% YoY), EV 12.3만대 
(+75% YoY)로 구성됨. 미국 HEV 판매가 전년 동기 대비 118% 증가하며 
시장 성장률을 약 4배 상회했고, 특히 서유럽 EV 판매가 전년 대비 163% 
증가함. 단 26.3Q 중 원화가 빠르게 강세로 전환되며 원/달러, 원/유로 
평균 환율은 전분기 대비 각 5.2%, 6.2% 하락함에 따른 – 효과 관찰됨. 
② Q: 26.3Q 도매 판매는 84.7만대 (+7.9% YoY)를 기록했고, 북미, 유럽, 
인도 등 주요 시장에서 고른 성장 나타남. 26.3Q 소매 판매 또한 82.9만대 
(+6.9% YoY)로 글로벌 산업 수요 (-6% YoY)를 상회. 북미는 HEV, 내수와 
유럽은 EV, 인도는 신형 셀토스와 소넷을 중심으로 판매가 확대됨 (이하 
26.3Q 권역별 도매 판매 YoY, 국내: +2.2%, 북미: +7.6%. 유럽: +19.3%. 
인도: +38.5%). 이란 사태 장기화로 아중동 도매 판매는 전년 동기 대비 
28.4% 감소했으나, 수요가 견조한 중남미와 CIS로 물량을 전환하며 
감소분을 상당 부분 만회함. 연간 도매 판매 가이던스 (335만대)를 유지함. 
③ C: 26.3Q 관세 비용은 약 0.7조원으로 추정되며, 전년 동기 대비 
5,000억원 감소함 (25.2Q 0.7조원, 25.3Q 1.2조원, 25.4Q 1.0조원, 26.1Q 
0.8조원). 반면 인센티브 증가 및 가격 인하 7,270억원, 서유럽 EV 비중 
확대에 따른 믹스 악화 4,650억원, 원자재 가격 상승 영향 3,680억원 등 
총 1.6조원의 감익 요인이 발생함. 이에 26.3Q 매출원가율은 84.1%로 
전년 동기 대비 3.0%p 상승함 (인센티브 +1.9%p, 재료비 +1.2%p). 다만 
26.3Q 기말 환율 급락하며 판매보증충당부채 환입 규모가 축소되었고 
판매보증비 약 9,000억원 감소. 판매관리비율은 10.1%로 3.7%p 개선됨. 
26.3Q 기아 순이익은 1.29조원으로 전년 동기 대비 9.6% 감소했다. 원화 강세에 
따라 외환환산 관련 이익이 전년 동기 540억원 이익에서 3,718억원으로 손실 
전환됐고, 현대모비스 등 관계사 이익 감소로 지분법손익 또한 360억원 감소했기 
때문이다 (현대차 순이익 감소 논리와 동일). 1) 산업 수요 감소와 중동 판매 차질에도 
권역별 파워트레인 대응을 통해 그룹사 대비 가지고 있는 외형 성장성을 증명했으나, 
2) 유럽 EV 판매 확대 과정에서 발생한 인센티브와 믹스 악화라는 비용 부담, 원자재 
가격 상승이 관세 감소 효과를 대부분 반납했고, 3) 분기 중 급격한 원화 강세가 
더해지며 시장 기대를 크게 하회하는 수익성을 기록했다는 것이 26.3Q 실적 총평이다.

실적 발표 후 주가 급락: 성장에도 불구하고 수익성에 대한 우려  
26.10.30 실적 발표 당일 기아 주가는 11.7% 하락했다. 하루 전 발표된 그룹사 실적 
발표와 상반된 모습이었다. 시장이 이번 실적에서 확인한 우려는 아래와 같다. 첫째, 
성장과 이익의 괴리다. 관세 영향을 제외한 26.3Q 영업이익은 2.52조원으로 전년 
동기(2.70조원) 대비 오히려 1,770억원 감소했다. 판매 증가에 따른 물량 효과 
(+4,420억원)와 환율 효과 (+5,290억원)가 발생했음에도, 인센티브와 믹스 악화로 이를 
대부분 반납한 것이다. 기아의 외형 성장 상당 부분이 유럽 EV 판매 증가에서 나오는데, 
해당 시장에서는 중국 EV 업체와의 경쟁으로 공격적인 프로모션이 불가피하다. 26.3Q 
유럽 권역에서의 중소형 EV 판매가 사업 계획을 크게 웃돌며 EV 믹스는 13.1%에서 
14.8%로 확대됐다. 시장은 이를 M/S를 위해 수익성을 훼손하고 있다고 받아들이는 
것이다. 둘째, 당장 26.4Q에도 유의미한 수익성 개선이 없을 것이라는 점이다. 기아는 
26.3Q 컨퍼런스 콜에서 4Q 영업이익률이 3Q 수준과 크게 다르지 않을 것이며, 연초 
제시한 연간 가이던스 (영업이익 10.2조원, +8.3% OPM)에 다소 미달할 것이라고 
밝혔다. 중동 분쟁의 영향이 최소 연말까지 이어지고, 원자재와 물류비 부담 또한 
지속될 것이 회사의 판단이다. 무엇보다 원/달러 환율이 26.2Q 평균 약 1,505원에서 
26.3Q 약 1,430원, 현재 1,320~1,340원 수준까지 하락하며 이에 따른 - 영향이 커질 
수 있다. 셋째, 그룹사와의 이익 체력 비교다. 현대차는 26.3Q 파업과 생산 차질로 도매 
판매가 11.3% 감소했음에도 원가 절감을 통해 영업이익이 5.5% 증가했고, 시장 
기대를 상회했다. 반면 기아는 판매가 7.9% 증가했음에도 영업이익이 컨센서스를 20% 
이상 하회했다. 26.3Q 양사 영업이익 격차는 약 8,600억원까지 벌어진 상황이다.  
단기 노이즈를 제외한다면 기아의 기업 가치는 여전히 높다  
그럼에도 당사는 기아의 EV 전략, 낮은 수익성을 이유로 중장기 기업 가치에 대한 
기대를 낮출 이유는 없다고 판단한다. 첫째, 외부 요인을 걷어내면 본원적인 수익 
창출력은 유지되고 있다. 기아는 26.3Q 영업이익률을 낮춘 외부 요인을 원자재 1.2%p, 
중동 판매 차질 0.7%p, 환율 1.7%p 등 총 3.6%p로 분석했다. 즉 중동 분쟁이 
해소되면 상당 부분이 사라질 일회성 요인이다. 둘째, EV 수익성 개선 경로는 HEV가 
걸어온 길과 같다. 기아는 HEV 역시 도입 초기에는 수익을 내지 못했으나 현재는 ICE에 
버금가는 수익성을 내고 있다며, 26~27년 현재의 EV 전략을 유지하겠다고 밝혔다. 
유럽 ICE 시장은 26.3Q 전년 동기 대비 약 20% 축소된 반면 EV 시장은 50% 이상 
성장하고 있다. 전환기에 스케일을 확보하지 못하면 EV 마진을 개선할 방법도 없다. 
기아는 향후 3년간 배터리, 모터, 히트펌프 등 주요 부품에서 연 10% 수준의 원가 
절감을 추진하고, 2~3년 내 EV를 HEV와 같은 수익성 경로에 올려놓겠다는 계획이다. 
셋째, 중장기 성장 계획을 수정하지 않았다. 기아는 27년 사업 계획이 CID 당시 제시한 
숫자와 큰 차이가 없을 것이며, 중장기 영업이익률 10% 목표도 유지했다. IAA 시행에 
대비해 슬로바키아 공장에서 EV를 전량 생산하고 ICE 국내 수출 방안도 검토 중이다. 
주주환원 측면에서도 TSR 35%를 지키고, 자사주 매입보다 배당을 우선하겠다는 
입장이다. 10/30 종가와 최소 DPS 기준만으로도 배당수익률은 6.3%에 달하고 있다.

기아 판매관리비 (좌) 판매관리비율 (우)
  26.4Q 기아 매출액 31.8조원, 영업이익 2.2조원 전망 
기아 26.4Q 실적을 매출액 31.8조원 (+13.2% YoY), 영업이익 2.2조원 (+20.8% YoY, 
+7.0% OPM)으로 전망한다. 26.4Q 판매 성장세는 이어지고, 3Q에 집중 반영된 원자재 
부담 일부 완화와 계절적 성수기 효과로 3Q 대비 수익성은 소폭 개선될 것이다. 다만 
1) 원화 강세에 따른 평균 환율 하락, 2) 유럽 EV 인센티브의 높은 수준 유지 (미국은 
할부 금리 상승으로 QoQ 약 200달러 증가, 유럽은 전분기와 유사), 3) 중동 판매 
차질과 원자재, 물류비 부담 지속을 감안하면 의미 있는 반등은 어렵다. 이는 4Q 
영업이익률이 3Q 수준과 크게 다르지 않을 것이라는 회사의 언급과도 궤를 같이한다. 
기아 목표주가 150,000원으로 하향  
기아 목표주가를 26.4Q 추정치 하향을 반영해 150,000원으로 하향한다. 기아의 판매 
경쟁력은 금번 분기에도 증명됐다. 다만 EV 판매 확대가 수익성 개선으로 연결된다는 
점이 숫자로 확인되기 전까지, 시장은 기아의 성장에 할인율을 적용할 것이다. 중동 
분쟁 해소와 EV 원가 절감이 맞물리는 27년이 기아가 수익성을 증명할 시점이 될 
것이며, 6%를 상회하는 배당수익률이 그때까지 주가의 하방을 지지할 것으로 판단된다.

 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

## Document 219: [000270] 기아(000270) 라인업 확대의 성과와 주주환원에 주목기아(000270) 라인업 확대의 성과와 주주환원에 주목라인업 확대의 성과와 주주_bf4ab2b6

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000270] 기아(000270) 라인업 확대의 성과와 주주환원에 주목기아(000270) 라인업 확대의 성과와 주주환원에 주목라인업 확대의 성과와 주주_bf4ab2b6.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-11-25
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

00

기아 (000270/KS) 
라인업 확대의 성과와 주주환원에 주목 
2027 년 영업이익 7.96 조 원 전망 
2027 년 실적은 매출액 124.3 조 원(YoY +1.0%), 영업이익 7.96 조 원(YoY -
5.3%, OPM 6.4%), 지배주주순이익 6.97 조 원(YoY -0.2%)으로 전망한다. 우호적
이었던 환율 효과가 약해지는 가운데, 높아진 원재료 투입가격과 유럽을 중심으로 한 
판매 경쟁도 수익성에 부담으로 남는다. 판매를 지키기 위한 인센티브 확대까지 감안하
면 물량 증가가 그대로 이익 증가로 이어지기는 어렵다. 다만 이미 투입한 신차의 연간 
판매 기여와 미국, 유럽의 라인업 확대가 비용 부담을 일부 상쇄하면서, 하반기에는 전
년 동기 대비 증익으로 돌아설 것으로 본다.

지역별로 갖춘 제품, PBV 로 넓어지는 고객 
2027 년에는 이미 출시한 신차의 판매를 늘려 제품과 설비에 투자한 성과를 매출과 이
익으로 연결하는 일이 중요하다. 미국에서는 텔루라이드 등 주력 SUV 와 확대된 하이
브리드 차종으로 수요에 대응한다. 전기차 전환을 서두르지 않는 고객에게도 선택지를 
제공하며, 이 가운데 현지 생산 차종은 완성차 수입에 따른 관세 부담을 줄이는 데도 도
움이 된다. 유럽에서는 EV2·EV4 등 전기차와 신형 셀토스로 판매 기회를 넓힌다. 기
존 슬로바키아 공장을 활용해 전기차를 공급할 수 있지만, 중국 업체와의 경쟁 속에서 
판촉 부담을 관리하는 과제도 남아 있다.

PBV 는 PV5 에 이어 PV7 을 투입하며 물류 및 운송업체의 용도별 수요에 대응할 계
획이다. 차량 크기와 적재 수요에 맞춘 제품에 판매와 정비망을 더하면 고객과의 거래
를 넓힐 수 있다. 미국 하이브리드의 판매 확대, 유럽 전기차의 안착, PBV 의 고객 확보
는 각각 이익에 기여하는 속도가 다르다. 따라서 판매 증가와 함께 제품 구성의 변화, 
신규 차종의 초기 비용이 수익성에 미치는 영향을 살펴야 한다.

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 12 만 원으로 커버리지 개시 
투자의견 매수, 목표주가 12 만 원으로 커버리지를 개시한다. 당사 12 개월 선행 EPS 에 
최근 1 년 평균 P/E 6.5 배를 적용했다. 연간 감익 부담은 남아 있지만, 지역별 수요에 
대응할 제품과 생산기반은 확대돼 있다. 낮아진 밸류에이션 부담을 감안하면, 이미 갖
춘 라인업의 판매 성과와 총주주환원율 35% 이상의 정책에 주목해야 한다는 판단이
다. 
2026-11-25

모빌리티/피지컬AI. 권순우 / soonwoo@sks.co.kr / 02-3773-9592

## Document 282: AI抛售拖累美股盘中重挫，逢低买盘力撑道指转涨，避险需求推升美债，贵金属深V反转

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI抛售拖累美股盘中重挫，逢低买盘力撑道指转涨，避险需求推升美债，贵金属深V反转.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

纳指100⼀度较历史峰值回撤20%，逢低买盘收窄跌幅，最终纳指100收跌1%，纳指跌
0.65%，道指涨0.3%。芯⽚指数跌0.65%。英伟达跌3.9%。美光科技跌4.5%。绩后安霸
跌20%，惠普涨11%。10年期美债收益率盘中降超10个基点。现货⻩⾦盘中跌破3500美
元、⼀度跌近4%，后转涨。布油收涨3.5%。
摘要：
美国股市在感恩节假期前⼣剧烈震荡，AI相关股票抛售⼀度拖累纳指深跌、进⼊技术性熊市，但
逢低买盘介⼊，使主要指数显著脱离⽇内低点。科技股仍是压⼒核⼼，道指则逆势收⾼。
周三标普500指数跌0.25%⾄7183.08点，纳斯达克综合指数跌0.65%⾄23579.44点，道琼斯⼯
业平均指数涨0.31%⾄50173.90点。
板块层⾯，科技板块领跌，材料与房地产板块表现居前。节前交投清淡，成交量低于平均⽔
平，美股周四休市、周五提前收盘。
抛售主线仍是AI交易降温。英伟达下跌，并拖累半导体板块⾛弱。投资者同时消化美国新的关税
提案，以及强于初值的三季度经济增⻓和10⽉通胀数据，这些因素强化了对美联储维持鹰派政

策的担忧。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

数据⾯上，10⽉个⼈消费⽀出（PCE）价格指数同⽐上涨3.8%，持续⾼于美联储2%的⽬标，市
场仍预计美联储12⽉将加息。
债市成为资⾦避⻛港，10年期美债收益率下⾏7基点。⼤宗商品⽅⾯，WTI原油涨2.5%⾄每桶
84.13美元，现货⾦涨0.5%⾄每盎司3575.16美元。美元指数基本持平，⽐特币涨0.7%⾄
60237.01美元。
纳指100盘中跌⼊技术性熊市，逢跌买盘推动道指转涨
周三美股⾛出⼀轮V型：早盘抛售由宏观数据和政策消息触发，午后在节前清淡的成交⾥，逢跌
买盘把指数从低点拉回，收盘三⼤指数分化。
据华尔街⻅闻，美国10⽉核⼼个⼈消费⽀出价格指数同⽐升3.3%，⽐市场预期⾼0.1个百分点，
为2023年11⽉以来最⾼；三季度实际GDP年化增速由初值3.4%上修⾄4.3%，个⼈消费⽀出环
⽐升0.3%，低于预期的0.4%。数据公布后，交易员维持对12⽉加息的定价。
开盘后卖压集中释放，道指⼀度跌超1,100点、跌幅2.2%。
纳指跌4.6%，标普500跌3.2%；纳指100最低到24,970.66点，较10⽉6⽇的纪录收盘⾼位回撤

20%，短暂进⼊技术性熊市。芝商所E-mini期权在开盘混乱时⼀度暂停交易。

### Page 3

交易台把反弹部分归因于“⼤空头”的博客，他称组合暂时⼏乎全部做多，并已削减纳指100看跌
仓位；也有交易台提到美国联邦通信委员会授权SpaceX部署卫星⽤于太空数据中⼼的消息。两
条说法都出现在成交极淡的时段，买盘来源难以确认。
美股⽇内低点附近成交量出现⼤幅飙升。
尾盘买盘把罗素2000推⾼0.71%，纳指100收于26,046.90点、跌0.64%，道指抹去全部盘中跌
幅并收涨。标普500剔除AI的组合当⽇录得上涨，⼴义AI篮⼦仍然收跌。
Aptus Capital Advisors股票主管David Wagner表⽰，市场在AI与⾮AI股票之间⾼度分化，眼下
是情绪驱动的抛售；如果市场开始计⼊过多的加息，情绪⾯的担忧有可能转为对经济增⻓的担
忧。
Wealthspire Advisors⾼级副总裁兼顾问Oliver Pursche表⽰，市场⾯临多重潜在阻⼒，但投资
者也已形成“逢低买⼊”的条件反射。他称，⼴泛抛售往往会“⼀并抛掉好资产”，⼀些投资者正在
利⽤这种错配。
英伟达承压，费城半导体指数仍收跌

围绕AI板块的压⼒来⾃多个⽅向。中期选举⺠主党胜出后，市场担忧AI⾏业将⾯临更严格的监管
审查；对AI投⼊规模与借贷成本上升的疑虑也在升温。个体的剧烈波动让投资者感到不安，进⽽
纷纷转向债券等避险资产。

### Page 4

英伟达⾃⾝的公司治理亦受到审视。标普全球评级正考虑将英伟达信⽤评级下调⼀档，理由是
对该公司的管理与治理状况存在担忧。
The Wealth Alliance总裁兼董事总经理Eric Diton表⽰，投资者先卖了再说，之后再问原因；英
伟达管理层动荡引发抛售，加息周期与科技股盈利增⻓⻅顶的担忧加重了跌势。
科技七巨头指数在开盘惨淡后有所反弹，但最终仍下跌超过1.5%。英伟达股价下跌3.9%，⾕歌
跌2.13%，亚⻢逊跌3.12%，微软跌1.25%。
半导体指数同步⾛低，费城半导体指数跌0.65%。美光科技跌4.53%，甲⻣⽂跌5.13%。
安 霸 跌19.77% 公 司 第 三 财 季 营 收 和 利 润 都 不 及 预 期 ， 当 季 指 引 也 低 于 市 场 估 计 。 惠 普 涨
11.49%，第四财季营收和利润均⾼于分析师预期，业绩公布后买盘延续到收盘。
IBM涨3.33%，家得宝涨2.14%，赛富时涨2.04%，宣伟涨2.40%。这四只股票所在⾏业对利率
和AI资本开⽀的敏感度较低，抵消了科技股下跌对道指的影响，IBM、赛富时和宣伟对道指转涨

的贡献最⼤。
亚德诺涨6.44%，德州仪器涨4.55%，⾼通涨2.24%。前⼀⽇亚德诺因下⼀财季利润指引低于预
期跌17.37%，德州仪器跌11.25%，两家公司当天出现回补。
⾼盛交易员John Flood表⽰，AI股票净敞⼝占全球净敞⼝的⽐重降到2025年9⽉以来最低，多空
⽐降到2024年10⽉以来最低，AI股票空头敞⼝占全球总敞⼝的⽐重处在多年⾼位。这些数字显
⽰对冲基⾦已经减了AI敞⼝。

### Page 5

德意志银⾏的Jim Reid表⽰，过去24⼩时避险情绪明显回归，市场当前的表现是板块轮动，上
周末那种不分⽅向的全⾯抛售没有延续。
避险买盘推动美债全线上涨，数据中⼼信⽤利差创纪录
10年期美债收益率收于4.862%，跌6.8个基点；2年期收于4.6477%，跌3.5个基点；30年期收
于5.251%，跌6.9个基点。
早盘收益率⼀度全线下跌逾10个基点，10年期触及9⽉9⽇以来最低，尾盘随股市反弹脱离⽇
低。
440亿美元7年期国债发⾏结果平稳，投标倍数2.43倍，中标收益率⽐发⾏前交易⽔平⾼约1个基
点。担保隔夜融资利率期货曲线⼤幅⽜平，交易员削减了对2027年加息幅度的押注。

### Page 6

⾼盛数据中⼼信⽤违约互换篮⼦收于523.5个基点，创纪录⾼位并保持到收盘；超⼤规模云⼚商
的同类合约盘中创纪录宽后回到持平。
Fort Washington Investment Advisors⾼级投资组合经理Dan Carter表⽰，当天债市上涨来⾃股
市⾛弱引发的避险买盘。
凯投宏观市场经济学家Joe Maher表⽰，科技股引发的抛售还会持续，会促使投资者下调利率预
期，资⾦继续涌向债券避险，他预计未来⼀年美国国债仍将⾛强。
中东供应⻛险推升油价，美元回落与贵⾦属V型反转
⼤宗商品⽅⾯，能源和贵⾦属上涨。WTI原油上涨2.5%，报每桶84.13美元。布伦特原油涨幅
3.51%，升⾄⼀周多以来⾼位。

### Page 7

油价在债市⾛强的同时独⽴上⾏，主因中东地缘⻛险再度升级。库存数据提供额外⽀撑。美国
能源信息署（EIA）报告显⽰，原油库存增加111万桶，低于市场预期的200万桶。交易员同时关
注周⽇的OPEC+会议，该组织预计将审议产能审计结果。
美元指数在纽约汇市尾市收于103.666，跌0.14%；欧元升⾄1.0980美元，英镑升⾄1.3122美
元，美元兑⽇元持平于159.21，美元兑瑞郎降⾄0.8309。
美东时间11点前后美元先出现⼀轮集中卖盘，⼀度较⽇⾼跌超0.5%。
随后，股市在美东时间11:45左右突然触底，⽽⻩⾦和加密货币也随之飙升。

### Page 8

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
现货⻩⾦⾛V收涨0.5%，盘中⼀度跌破3,500美元、随后反弹逾150美元。现货⽩银回到40.91美
元，涨0.37%；铜涨0.47%⾄5.39美元。
⽐特币上涨0.7%，报60237.01美元，以太币上涨0.6%，报1688.59美元。

Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX tăng điểm trong tuần qua, +9.57 điểm tương ứng +0.55%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Tập đoàn công nghiệp, Tiện ích Điện và Bất động sản là các nhóm ngành có mức tăng 
rõ rệt nhất trong tuần qua. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với 
giá trị -810 tỷ đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, VCB và STB; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán 
ròng ở mức -13 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng -1,161 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua 
và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -12,049 hợp đồng. Tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng -2,033 
hợp đồng và đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -6,103 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam trải qua tuần hồi phục nhẹ với biến động tăng/giảm đan xen, thanh khoản 
ở mức thấp và nhà đầu tư nước ngoài tiếp tục đẩy mạnh bán ròng. Hai phiên giữa tuần 
chứng kiến sự hồi phục mạnh mẽ của thị trường, phản ứng trước thông tin NHNN có động 
thái hỗ trợ thanh khoản hệ thống ngân hàng để hạ lãi suất thông qua nghiệp vụ hoán đổi 
ngoại tệ, và mua vào dự trữ ngoại hối. Dù vậy, áp lực bán nhanh chóng quay trở lại ở 2 
phiên cuối tuần khi nhà đầu tư hoài nghi về triển vọng hạ lãi suất trong ngắn hạn, trong 
khi TTCK toàn cầu cũng có diễn biến kém khả quan trước đà hồi phục của giá dầu, số liệu 
CPI Mỹ cao hơn kỳ vọng, và diễn biến tiêu cực ở nhóm cổ phiếu AI.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tiếp tục hình thành thêm một mẫu nến spinning trên khung tuần, hàm ý tương 
quan cung cầu đang tạm thời cân bằng; trong khi diễn biến tăng nhẹ về điểm số nhưng đi 
kèm sự sụt giảm của thanh khoản cho thấy tín hiệu hồi phục mang tính phân hóa rõ nét. 
Trên khung ngày, nhịp bật tăng tích cực của chỉ số sau khi chạm đường biên dưới của mẫu 
hình tam giác đi ngang trung hạn đang cho dấu hiệu chững lại tại vùng kháng cự gần 
quanh 1.750, phản ánh lực cầu mặc dù được củng cố khá tốt ở vùng giá thấp nhưng thiếu 
sự đồng thuận khớp lên các vùng giá cao. Diễn biến giằng co có thể còn tiếp diễn, tuy 
nhiên khi xu hướng ngắn hạn vẫn đang nghiêng về chiều tăng, chỉ số được kỳ vọng sẽ tiếp 
tục hướng lên, thử thách lại các vùng cản phía trên. 
(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

## Document 319: Central banks_ a pause while waiting for more information (2026-10-29)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Central banks_ a pause while waiting for more information (2026-10-29).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 30 October 2026

EcoFlash

Central banks: a pause while waiting for more information

As expected, the Fed, the ECB, and the BoJ left their rates unchanged this week. This pause, however, does not mark 
the end of the cycle. The ECB’s tone is becoming more balanced, weighing inflationary risks against tightening financial 
conditions, but one final rate hike in December remains our base case. Across the Atlantic, Kevin Warsh describes an 
environment conducive to further tightening, while remaining cautious about the risk of a credit shock. In Japan, the 
lasting exit from the deflationary cycle is taking shape, and monetary policy adjustment is expected to continue 
gradually. We anticipate two rate hikes by the Fed by January and four by the BoJ by the second half of 2028.

Eurozone: A more balanced tone, but the course remains unchanged. The ECB’s statement marks a shift from September. 
It now explicitly acknowledges both upside risks to inflation (the magnitude and persistence of the energy shock) and downside 
pressures (tightening financial conditions). The statement that inflation would remain “well above target for an extended period” 
no longer appears. In essence, however, there is little change. A rate hike in December remains our base case scenario, widely 
anticipated and implicitly signaled by Christine Lagarde during the Q&A (“If the current situation is not called into question by the 
next set of economic data, the path is clear”). Lagarde presented the rise in sovereign yields as a global phenomenon that is 
tightening financial conditions; regarding the TPI, she stuck to the usual stance: the ECB will intervene if monetary policy 
transmission were to be hampered “by disorderly movements not justified by fundamentals”. By December, the indirect and 
second-round effects of the energy shock will be closely scrutinised. From our perspective, September’s producer prices offer 
clearer signals, but the increase remains limited to a few sectors: energy, electricity-intensive industries and electrical equipment, 
driven by IA1. For now, companies in the Eurozone, particularly in the services sector, appear to be absorbing the shock. The 
European Commission’s survey released this Thursday indicates a rise in the sales price index in October, which remains in line 
with recent months.

United States: The Fed navigates between risks. The FOMC pauses after September’s rate hike and maintains the federal 
funds target at 3.75%-4.0%. However, Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh outlined a landscape conducive to further tightening: stronger 
growth, “rather positive” labor market conditions, and, above all, inflation that is “too high.” As a result, two dissenting votes (out 
of 12) were cast in favor of a 25bps hike. But the committee also appears to fear the adverse consequences of tightening too 
quickly, which is why it is waiting to “accumulate more information.” AI is increasingly at the heart of monetary policymaking. In 
particular, K. Warsh warned against the risks of an abrupt tightening of interest rate conditions, including for the AI investment 
cycle - which triggered a steepening of the yield curve. He also announced that he would soon (on November 16) attend a hearing 
of the “Productivity and Employment” task force to “refine his views” on the implications of AI for the dual mandate. Governor Lisa 
Cook, who voted for a 25bps hike, had noted ahead of the committee meeting “ongoing inflationary pressures” and a risk that they 
might not “subside quickly”. In addition, K. Warsh announced that there would be fewer meetings resulting in rate decisions: 7 in 
2027 and 6 in 2028, compared with 8 today. The change is expected to take effect starting in the fall of 2027. The meetings freed 
up by this change would allow the committee to focus on “strategic deliberations” and long-term issues.

Japan: A status quo, but a tightening cycle set to continue as the economy emerges from deflation. The Bank of Japan 
decided, in line with our forecasts and the consensus, to keep its policy rate target steady at 1.25% at its October 29-30 meeting. 
One dissenting vote called for a rate hike (25bps), contrasting with the two dovish dissenting votes (opposed to a rate hike) at the 
previous meeting. As in the US, the decision is justified by the need to further analyse risks to economic activity, prices, and 
financial conditions. Above all, the central bank’s outlook for economic activity and prices marks a turning point: it explicitly states 
that “core inflation has reached a level generally in line with price stability” and that it is “expected to remain there.” Governor Ueda 
refined this by noting that it is now necessary to “anchor” inflation at this level before declaring the target achieved. Furthermore, 
the emphasis on “upside risks to prices” and the fact that any rise in core inflation is now deemed “undesirable” indicate that 
monetary tightening will continue.

The monetary tightening cycle continues. The next steps are likely to be more significant in the United States, where inflation 
has exceeded the target for nearly six years and coexists with a favorable outlook for growth and employment. In the Eurozone, 
the rise in inflation is primarily due to a supply shock in energy costs (though it is also fueled by demand in the United States). It 
continues to have little impact on measures of core inflation, including those tracked by the ECB, such as the HICP; and 
households’ long-term inflation expectations remain anchored. The degree of monetary tightening also differs. In real terms, the 
ECB’s current rates (the deposit facility rate deflated by the core CPI) hover around 0%, a level that more or less corresponds to 
estimates of the natural rate. The Fed, meanwhile, remains in accommodative territory: the real rate (Fed Funds deflated by Core

1 This is a point underscored by the first PPI releases for September (Spain, Italy).

### Page 2

PCE) is at around 0.8%, 40bps below the committee’s implied long-term level (1.2%). Japan remains an outlier: the rise in rates 
preceded the inflationary shock and, until now, has been more a matter of normalization. It is therefore more extensive and spread 
out over time. The central bank is adjusting to the new inflation regime and remains cautious in its exit from accommodative 
policies. The starting point is significantly lower, with real rates still negative, but the adjustment cycle should ultimately bring it to 
the upper end of the neutral range estimated by the BoJ (1.1%-2.5%). We therefore anticipate one final rate hike by the ECB in 
December, two more hikes by the Fed, first in December and then in January, and four spaced-out hikes by the BoJ through the 
second half of 2028.

Anis Bensaidani   
Economist, United States, Japan 
anis.bensaidani@bnpparibas.com

Guillaume Derrien 
Economist, Europe 
guillaume.a.derrien@bnpparibas.com

## Document 362: Hedge funds like gilts, but not the risks

Source PDF: all report pdf/Hedge funds like gilts, but not the risks.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-27
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Hedge funds are increasingly absorbing gilt supply through
leveraged trading strategies but passing interest-rate risk to
other investors. The Bank of England is looking at policy
measures to mitigate the associated risks. Should hedge funds
exit such trades, gilts would likely underperform swaps as gilts
move to more expensive balance sheets
Michiel Tukker
Hedge funds are absorbing more gilt supply, but not the underlying interest-
rate risk, leaving yields vulnerable to further upward pressure

Gilt demand increasingly relies on more
price-sensitive buyers
The Bank of England’s moves to unwind its bond portfolio and
persistent government deﬁcits continue to increase the supply of
gilts that markets need to absorb. Meanwhile, demand from
traditional buyers such as UK pension funds has softened. Gilts
must also compete with a growing pool of sovereign debt globally,
with expanding US Treasury issuance and ﬁnancing needs tied to
the AI capex boom helping to keep global yields elevated.

### Page 2

Domestic non-bank ﬁnancials, including asset managers and
hedge funds, now account for almost 20% of gilt holdings, up from
just 5% in 2022. These investors tend to be much more price-
sensitive than other buyers and only step in when yields are
deemed attractive. Pension funds used to be important buyers of
gilts to match their long-dated liabilities. Their role is shrinking as
the sector transitions from deﬁned beneﬁt to deﬁned contribution
schemes.

Price-sensitive buyers have had to step in
to absorb QT supply
Hedge funds are getting paid for balance
sheet, not for interest rate risk
Hedge funds have shifted from big sellers to big buyers of gilts, but
their strategies appear designed to avoid the associated interest-
rate risk. Since quantitative tightening began, hedge funds have
begun holding signiﬁcant long positions in gilts. Most of this
exposure can likely be explained by futures-cash basis trades.

Unlike the gilt scarcity we saw during quantitative easing, the gilt
market is now ﬂooded with supply. This supply pressure widens the
spread between the pricing of futures and cash gilts. A simpliﬁed
explanation is that cash gilts take up much more balance sheet
capacity compared to gilt futures. As a result, many pension funds,
banks, and asset managers prefer futures to manage interest rate

can be as little as a few basis points.

Hedge funds face fewer balance sheet or regulatory constraints,
allowing them to exploit the pricing diﬀerence between cash gilts

and gilt futures. The trade is simple: buy cash gilts and short gilt
futures. Repo markets oﬀer the leverage to scale this trade, with
repo dealers lending cash against gilt collateral. BoE data highlights
a parallel increase in futures positions alongside rising repo activity.

The catch is that by hedging with short futures positions, hedge
funds aren’t actually absorbing the gilt market’s underlying

### Page 4

interest-rate risk.
Instead, the long futures positions are almost entirely held by asset
managers, including pension funds. For pension funds, much of this
exposure stems from regulatory requirements and the need to
match liabilities. But outside the pension and insurance sectors,
asset managers are likely to be quite price-sensitive in their futures
exposure.
Hedge funds pass the interest rate risk on
to other ﬁnancials
Hedge funds might therefore help to keep gilt spreads versus

futures (and swaps) tight by using their balance sheets to absorb
gilt supply. But buyers are still needed to absorb the market’s
underlying duration risk. That leaves us cautious that the term
premium embedded in the sterling curve, whether expressed
through gilts or swaps, could keep rising as increasing supply tests
the market’s capacity to absorb it.
Hedge funds have increased their footprint
in the eurozone and US too
In the euro area, hedge funds’ average net repo borrowing against

government bonds stood at around €70 billion this year (c.1% of
the government bond market), below the roughly £80 billion
recorded in the gilt market (c.3% of the gilt market). The European
Central Bank noted a signiﬁcant unwinding of hedge fund positions

### Page 5

in March when the Iran conﬂict triggered a rise in rate volatility. This
also coincided with a signiﬁcant widening of European government
bond (EGB) spreads. Since then, the volatility in EGB spreads has
remained elevated, although cause and eﬀect are diﬃcult to
pinpoint. In any case, the ECB has recently identiﬁed hedge funds'
material role in EGB trades as a potential ﬁnancial stability risk.
The US Treasury futures basis trade has also been a recurring focus

of the Federal Reserve’s ﬁnancial stability assessments. The latest
estimate suggests the futures basis trade accounts for a total of
$830bn of hedge funds' US Treasury positions (also around 3% of
the market). Part of the sharp sell-oﬀ in US Treasuries in March 2020
has been attributed to the unwinding of basis trades. While the
precise impact on yields is diﬃcult to quantify, the substantial
growth in these positions since then has increased the potential for

ﬁnancial stability risks.
Headwinds to the basis trade can trigger a
sell-off in government bonds
Because hedge funds provide substantial balance-sheet capacity
and liquidity to bond markets, unwinding these positions risks
putting upward pressure on yields. Other investors are unlikely to
step in unless bond valuations become suﬃciently attractive.
Banks, for example, face high balance sheet costs, and potentially
leverage ratio constraints, limiting their appetite. In eﬀect, we would

expect gilts to underperform swaps in such a scenario, pushing gilt
yields higher.
Leveraged gilt positions that rely on repo markets could face
headwinds if system liquidity tightens. Higher repo rates make
funding more expensive and could reduce the attractiveness of
speculative positions. Higher regulatory haircuts could also force
unwinding of leverage as the trade becomes less attractive.
Another risk stems from sharp moves in interest rates. A sudden

rise in yields could trigger margin calls on hedge funds’ short
futures positions, forcing them to liquidate gilt holdings. The
dynamics would resemble the 2022 gilt crisis, when an unfunded
mini-budget ﬂoated by Prime Minister Liz Truss’ government
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.

our Cookie policy.

### Page 6

triggered market turmoil, widespread margin calls, and forced
selling by pension funds.
Since then, the BoE has strengthened liquidity backstops for
pension funds, reducing the risk of a repeat episode. Hedge funds,
however, have less direct access to such support mechanisms. That
said, as it did during the 2022 gilt crisis, the Bank could step in and
purchase gilts in the secondary market to prevent a disorderly
unwind.
Another complexity is that hedge funds often pursue multiple

strategies, so turmoil in other markets could spill over into gilts. If a
hedge fund faces losses or margin calls on an unrelated strategy, a
derisking of portfolios might also force an unwinding of gilt-related
trades.
Increased market volatility could trigger an
unwinding of hedge fund positions

The BoE is currently reviewing possible regulation to mitigate the
risks surrounding leveraged gilt trades, which could make basis
trades less attractive. For example, imposing minimum haircuts on
bilateral trades would reduce the leverage hedge funds can obtain.
Alternatively, requiring more central clearing in repo markets could

help reduce counterparty credit risk. But this could also increase
margining costs for hedge funds.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
• US markets were closed for the Thanksgiving public holiday. 
European equities rebounded on Thursday following two 
consecutive sessions of losses. The Euro Stoxx 50 rose 
2.0%, while the FTSE 100 gained 1.8%, its strongest 
advance in more than three weeks.
• Asian equities closed higher following a late recovery on 
Wall Street on Wednesday, though gains were pared after 
reports that Nvidia had told AI hardware manufacturers to 
halt server shipments to Southeast Asia. The Nikkei 225 
gained 0.9%, while the Kospi rose 0.3%, supported by a 
significant upgrade to the Bank of Korea’s 2027 growth 
outlook, as the central bank delivered on its widely expected 
25bp rate hike. The Hang Seng outperformed with a 1.2% 
gain, while the CSI 300 was little changed, up 0.1%. The 
ASX 200 rose 0.8%, led by gains in mining and energy 
stocks, after fresh US tariff proposals spared Australia.
• European yields were broadly steady, although moves were 
more pronounced at the UK front end, with the 2-year gilt 
yield rising 7bps compared with a 2bp increase in the 10-
year, while the 10-year Bund yield was also up 2bps.
• ACGB yields were broadly unchanged, with the 3-year down 
1bp at 4.48% and the 10-year steady at 4.82%. Stronger-
than-expected Q3 capex data did little to shift RBA pricing, 
with the first rate cut still priced by August.
• Currency moves were modest. The USD was little changed, 
with the DXY gaining 0.1% to 103.77. AUD/USD was also 
broadly steady, up 0.1% at 0.6634. Elsewhere, EUR/USD 
was unchanged at 1.0972, while USD/JPY rose 0.1% to 
159.26. The AUD gained 0.4% against the NZD.
• In oil markets, Houthi rebels attacked Saudi Arabia’s Jazan 
refinery and Red Sea ports, with the Saudi-led coalition 
subsequently announcing it had destroyed the ballistic 
missile launch platform used in the attack. OPEC+ is 
reportedly considering extending current output quotas 
into January amid uncertainty surrounding the Iran war, 
ahead of an online meeting on Sunday. Brent futures rose 
2.1% to US$96.6/bbl. Copper rebounded 1.9% from an 
11-month low, supported by a stabilisation in the AI trade, 
while aluminium gained 2.1%. Gold futures fell 0.6% to 
US$3,570/oz, approaching a 15-month low as markets 
continued to price a more hawkish Fed outlook. 
International Data
Spain’s Pablo Hernandez de Cos has been announced as 
Christine Lagarde’s successor as ECB President after a summit 
in Brussels. The European Parliament and ECB Governing 
Council will formally give their support for the decision in 
coming months. Pablo Hernandez de Cos previously served on 
the ECB’s Governing Council while head of Spain’s central bank 
between 2018 and 2024.

October’s ECB meeting minutes, where the stance of policy 
was maintained, showed members largely agreed the inflation 
outlook had deteriorated, and that “further tightening was likely 
necessary” unless the inflation outlook improved significantly.
Domestic Data

## Document 403: Q3 Growth in advanced economies_ solid despite shocks (2026-10-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Q3 Growth in advanced economies_ solid despite shocks (2026-10-23).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 23 October 2026

EcoFlash

Q3 Growth in advanced economies: solid despite shocks

Growth looks to have held up in Q3 despite successive shocks (see our analysis), as it did in 2025 and H1 2026. A longer 
Middle East conflict has fed into more persistent inflation; drought has worsened in many countries, and interest rates 
have risen further – at the short end, with both the ECB and the Fed raising policy rates in September, and even more at 
the long end. However, our nowcasts show that growth is bending without breaking in most advanced economies. GDP 
figures (due on 29 October in the US and 30 October for the euro area and its member states) are expected to confirm 
that. In the Euro area, momentum looks stable (excluding Ireland, whose GDP is volatile). In Germany, growth has been 
uninterrupted since Q4 2025, while France is likely to have returned to growth after stagnating in H1. In the US, growth 
is likely to have gathered pace, according to the GDP Now, still driven by AI, but increasingly by other sectors as well.

Euro area: resilience beneath the surface. Our nowcast points to growth slowing to 0.3% in q/q, though the pace is stable 
excluding Ireland (Irish GDP is volatile and had markedly boosted the Q2 figure). Excluding Ireland, growth should hold at its H2 
average, a sign of the bloc’s underlying resilience. Also, momentum has broadened to a growing number of sectors (see our 
analysis). While industrial production stalled over the summer, services remained buoyant. The services PMI rebounded sharply 
in Q3 and sits firmly in expansion territory (53.0 in September). Improving job-related survey data (PMI and European Commission 
surveys) and a stable unemployment rate (6.4% in August) hint at more hiring in Q3 – a support for household consumption that 
partly offsets purchasing-power losses from persistent inflation. Corporate investment is underpinned by buoyant new investment 
lending (8.7% y/y In August, 12-month cumulative). 
France: growing, nevertheless. The succession of shocks does not appear to have prevented France from returning to 
growth in Q3, at 0.2% q/q, according to our nowcast. One-off factors accounted for the stall in activity in H1. In Q3, the drought 
worsened and industrial production remained subdued. However, market services production rebounded in July and August, 
notably in business services, restaurants, digital and real estate. The latter points to residential investment recovering after a weak 
H1. Corporate investment likely followed, as reflected in buoyant credit flows and AI-related investment. Household consumption 
is likely to have improved slightly on higher electric vehicle sales.  
Euro area  France

Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas  Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas

Germany: growth still solid. The H1 rebound in activity is moderating but continues. Our nowcast points to a fourth straight 
quarter of growth in Q3, at 0.2% q/q (after 0.3% in Q4 2025, 0.4% in Q1 2026, and 0.3% in Q2). Manufacturing activity eased 
slightly, according to the September PMI survey (53.9, down 0.4 m/m). Government spending will remain growth-supportive, with 
defence and infrastructure outlays gaining traction. Infrastructure spending continues to underpin activity in public works, which 
stands at its highest since November 2021 (2.5% 3m/3m in August). Exports (4.4% y/y between January and August) remain the 
main growth engine, helped by a pivot towards Europe (7.2%). 
United States: an expansion undeterred by inflation. Growth is likely to have gathered pace in Q3. The Atlanta Fed’s GDP 
Now points to a 3.5% annualized growth rate (AR), close to our own 3.7% forecast (0.9% non-AR) and up from 2.2% in 
Q2. Domestic demand remains the engine. Consumption is weathering the pickup in inflation, while the AI-driven capex cycle 
remains robust – as reflected in retail sales and nondefence capital-goods orders (excl. aircraft). As a result, growth in final sales

### Page 2

to private domestic purchasers1 should print above 4% AR again. By contrast, external trade should remain a drag on growth, 
reflecting AI-related import needs. Survey data point to momentum that is both solid and broad-based. The ISM indices have 
remained firmly in growth territory (54.9 quarterly average for manufacturing and services alike). At the same time, they highlight 
persistent price and supply-chain pressures.

Germany   United States

Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas   Source: Refinitiv, Atlanta Fed, BNP Paribas

We had a solid (post-midterms) consumer sentiment report on

Padhraic Garvey, CFA and Benjamin Schroeder

The downtrend in yield to be frustrated by
Wednesday's PCE report
The US consumer conﬁdence report from the Conference Board is
worthy of note. It was conducted mostly after the midterm
elections, and in that sense reﬂects a degree of satisfaction among
the US electorate. We assert this not from a political preference
perspective, but from the reality that a signiﬁcant part of the
population wanted some political shake-up, and that is what they
got. There was eﬀectively a 10-point rise across all three of the key

indices. The overall index is back above 100 for the ﬁrst time since
early 2025. That's the key one. And the Present Situation Index is
now some 30% above its reference level. That balances the
Expectations Index, which is more than 15% below neutral. Both of
these are important, but the present situation is dominating, as it
reﬂects contemporaneous consumer spending.
The thing is, bonds eﬀectively ignored this report. That's strange, as
the consumer is key to the overall economy. Treasuries, it seems,

are intent on testing the lower bounds for yields. Even the Nvidia
reversal on Monday was met by lower rather than higher yields.
Behind the scenes, there has been an increase in bearish positions
in derivatives. But clearly not enough to sway the overall market
just yet. Wednesday's core PCE should be a worry for Treasuries, as
a rise to 3.2% year-on-year is the central market expectation. And
the headline rate heads to 3.8% year-on-year. While it is true that

energy prices have come oﬀ and can help to tame headline
inﬂation rates ahead, that does little for core inﬂation rates, or, at
the very least, it will take a bit longer to feed through (e.g. airfares).
Conﬁrmation of these expectations should, by deﬁnition, not be a
surprise. But we feel that conﬁrmation will, in fact, be enough to
abort the downtrend in yields.
The eurozone gets dragged along by US
sentiment
While data such as Monday’s PMIs continue to paint a resilient

macro backdrop, the souring equity sentiment is dominating the
picture ahead of the US Thanksgiving holidays. It hasn’t helped that
the US government is reportedly close to announcing tariﬀs on
China, the EU and other trading partners. The 10y Bund yield is
close to the early November low, at just above 3.25%. Clearly, the
mood music will be bullish for rates as long as the equity gloom
continues. At the same time, oil prices have remained contained,

which has helped rates move lower over the past week. In the
eurozone, the cold spell is also seeing natural gas prices creep
higher, tempering the benign impact of lower oil prices.

(US$44bn).

## Document 434: Top of Mind_ Higher bond yields_ how concerning_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Top of Mind_ Higher bond yields_ how concerning_.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 31
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The Goldman Sachs Group, Inc.
Global bond yields have risen to multi-decade highs, yet growth and markets have
remained remarkably resilient. But with some pressures beginning to emerge (e.g. in
European sovereign bond markets), can this resilience last, or will more vulnerabilities
be revealed? We ask the IMF’s Tobias Adrian, Moody’s Analytics’ Mark Zandi, and GS
economists. Adrian is relatively sanguine about the economy’s resilience, though
notes downside risks, while GS economists expect only a modest growth drag from

higher rates, but a more consequential one if yields remain near current or higher
levels. Zandi is generally more concerned, describing the US economy as vulnerable
rather than resilient, and sees one-in-three odds of the economy going off the rails.
Meanwhile, GS strategists and analysts are not especially concerned about banks or
markets more broadly, but warn that further increases in yields or rate volatility could
test risk assets. Key to watch: whether the increasingly interconnected and leveraged

AI investment boom can continue to withstand higher rates.
Tobias Adrian, Special Advisor and former Director of Monetary and Capital Markets, IMF
“The global economy has demonstrated considerable resilience in the face of multiple severe shocks, and I would not be
surprised if it continues to shrug off higher yields, provided they do not keep rising sharply in a disorderly fashion. At the same
time, I do worry about downside risks.”
Mark Zandi, Chief Economist, Moody’s Analytics

election outcome likely to lead to further BRL appreciation.

We provide a brief snapshot on the most important economies for the global markets

### Page 3

El 
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 3 
Top of Mind Issue 152

Recent acceleration in the rise in global bond yields to multi-
decade highs has begun to raise questions about the 
implications for growth and markets. Both have remained 
remarkably resilient so far, with US growth in particular 
holding relatively firm and the S&P 500 and other equity 
markets near new all-time highs. But with pressure 
beginning to emerge—most notably in European sovereign 
bond markets—and yields looking unlikely to retreat soon, 
can that resilience last, or will more vulnerabilities be revealed? 
GS Head of US Rates Strategy William Marshall first lays out 
how we arrived at higher rates, underscoring the striking 
breadth of the rise, which he attributes mainly to 
fundamentals—strong growth, inflation risks, hawkish policy 
shifts, the ongoing investment cycle, and long-run fiscal 
risks—but notes that technical factors likely amplified the 
recent move. And while he ultimately expects yields to fall 
(with YE 10-year US Treasury yields of 4.75%/4.4% in 
2026/2027, respectively) as markets become more confident 
in easing inflation, he thinks elevated long-term yields should 
have some staying power, and sees continued upside risk 
from energy price and fiscal pressures.  
So, just how much of a threat do higher rates pose to the 
macro outlook? For answers, we first turn to Tobias Adrian, 
Special Advisor to the Managing Director and former 
Financial Counsellor and Director of the Monetary and Capital 
Markets Department at the IMF, Mark Zandi, Chief 
Economist of Moody’s Analytics, and GS US economists. 
Adrian sees a global economy that has so far responded to 
the yield shock much as it has to the many other major 
shocks of the past seven years: resiliently. He notes that the 
rise in yields has not significantly tightened financial 
conditions or, more importantly, disrupted market functioning 
or forced damaging deleveraging. He believes that resilience 
could persist as long as the move in yields doesn’t accelerate 
or become more disorderly. But he is concerned about 
vulnerabilities in the AI and technology complex, sovereign 
bond markets, and EMs—not to mention cyber risks—all of 
which leaves him “vigilant but cautiously optimistic.” 
GS US economists also characterize the US economy as 
resilient. Their expectation that the Fed will deliver just one 
more rate hike this year before cutting next year—consistent 
with Marshall’s expectation of lower Treasury yields—leads 
Pierfrancesco Mei to expect only a modest 0.2pp growth 
drag in 2027 from higher rates. But he finds that the drag 
could rise to a more consequential 0.5pp if yields remain near 
current levels (see pg. 9 for GS global economists Joseph 
Briggs’ and Johan Allen’s rules of thumb for the impact of 
higher yields on rate-sensitive areas across major DMs.) 
David Mericle and Mei also worry about the implications for 
the already-concerning US fiscal trajectory, which they 
believe is unlikely to trigger a classic debt crisis but is too risky 
to ignore.     
Zandi, for his part, is generally more concerned. While his 
baseline outlook is relatively sanguine, he describes the US 
economy as vulnerable rather than resilient and views the 
“distribution of possible outcomes as very flat and heavily 
skewed toward darker tail scenarios.” He sees the clearest 
softness in the labor market, with weak hiring and falling real 
wage growth for many workers weighing on consumer 
spending. And he takes little comfort from US growth 
continuing to run around 2%—roughly its potential rate—
noting that AI accounts for roughly one-third of that growth, 
leaving the non-AI economy growing at only around 1-1.5%, 
which he calls “a pretty punk economy.” Zandi also worries 
that the US fiscal situation is “as bad as I have ever seen it” 
and that higher rates are already inflicting damage on real 
estate markets and squeezing existing debtors. As these 
stresses mount, he sees elevated risk of something breaking 
in the financial system. All told, he puts the odds of the 
current vulnerabilities pushing the economy completely off 
the rails at around one-in-three.  
With banks historically at the center of the rate shocks and 
economic crises that Zandi is worried about, we then explore 
how much risk banks pose today. Adrian and GS Head of the 
Financials Equity Group Richard Ramsden both see little 
cause for alarm, explaining that higher rates should boost 
banks’ net interest income and revenues given their asset-
sensitive balance sheets. Ramsden also doesn’t expect a 
significant deterioration in credit quality absent a broader 
economic downturn and says that most banks should have 
excess capital even after accounting for rate-driven portfolio 
losses.   
But even if banks can weather higher rates, what about 
markets more broadly? GS strategists and analysts are not 
especially concerned, with Chief US Equity Strategist Ben 
Snider arguing that current rate levels shouldn’t prevent 
continued strong US earnings growth and the S&P 500 from 
climbing higher, Chief Credit Strategist Amanda Lynam 
viewing the headwind to corporate credit from higher rates 
as manageable amid the solid growth backdrop, and US 
Financials Analyst Alexander Blostein expecting the recent 
slowdown in private market activity to rebound once rates 
stabilize. GS multi-asset strategists Christian Mueller-
Glissmann and Andrea Ferrario add that higher yields have 
strengthened the strategic case for bonds in a multi-asset 
portfolio, though strong earnings continue to favor equities 
for now. But they all warn that if rate volatility remains 
elevated, and especially if yields continue to rise from here, 
risk assets could face mounting pressures.  
All that said, with the AI boom powering the economy and 
markets, everything may come down to whether higher rates 
will derail it. Snider views this as unlikely given the strong 
balance sheets of the firms investing in AI and the expected 
profitability of these investments, with Lynam also seeing a 
high bar to derail AI-related debt issuance. Adrian also 
maintains a positive baseline for the AI economy but is 
concerned about the circularity of investment, increased 
leverage, and the sustainability of revenue growth in the AI 
complex. And Zandi again seems the most worried, warning 
that “AI could become the part of the economy that breaks” 
if rates remain at current levels for much longer.  
Allison Nathan, Editor
